# Multi-Level Work Breakdown Structure (WBS)

> **Companion design document**. This document is a companion to
> [RFC-039 Compiler Architecture Refactoring](../../rfc/accepted/039-compiler-architecture.md). It
> expands the RFC’s phase sequence into **three-level tasks that can be committed and verified
> independently**.
>
> RFC-039’s main text is the sole authority for phase order and acceptance gates. This document is
> responsible only for decomposing the work into an executable granularity and registering conflicts
> that remain undecided.

**Reserved numbers (not reclaimed)**: P4 has no `4.7`/`4.8`, and P8 has no `8.1`. None of the three
has had a corresponding entry since the WBS was established—the entire history of this document has
zero hits for the three headings `**4.7` / `**4.8` / `**8.1` (verifiable with
`git log -S'**4.7' -- docs/src/dev/architecture/09-execution-wbs.md`), and no other repository
references exist. `8.1` corresponds precisely to phase 1 of `05` (test revival and completion): that
phase was split into P1 (`1.1`–`1.3`) and `8.8`, so P8’s numbering aligns with its phase numbers
(phase 0 → `8.0`, phase 2 → `8.2`, and so forth).

**Covered by this document**: Task decomposition, dependencies, parallelization groups, and binding
of acceptance criteria.

**Not covered by this document**: The design rationale for tasks (see `02`–`08`) or the argument for
phase order (see RFC-039).

**Registration convention (effective 2026-10-12)**: Every newly registered Level 2/Level 3 row
**must contain two fields**—“**Owning Phase**” (the P to which it truly belongs, which may differ
from its current section) and “**Blocks the Mainline**.” `Pending scheduling` is no longer the
default state; an unmarked task is considered **not schedulable**. Numbers must be **unique across
the entire document** (cross-track tasks use the track number, such as `029g.x`, and must not occupy
`N.x`). **Check-off/completion registration is based on “task ID + evidence (gate metrics, run ID,
issue number, file:line),” not commit hashes**—`pull --rebase` rewrites local hashes, so a
hard-coded hash inevitably becomes invalid (2026-10-13 example: `94ad03ce4`→`631eb3e3c`). Use
`git log` to look up commit hashes.

## Current State: Gaps Found During Decomposition

While consolidating the “Implementation Points” of eight documents into the master table, **five
hard gaps in the documentation system itself** were found. All have now been resolved:

| #   | Gap                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Resolution                                                                                                                                                           |
| --- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| G1  | **P5 has no implementation steps.** RFC-039 defines P5 (checker splitting) and its acceptance criterion (C1 zero-diff), but none of the seven documents describes how to perform the split. The directory tree in `01-routing.md` contains one line for `annotations.rs`, the only mention in the entire batch.                                                                     | P5’s steps are supplied by this document (see below).                                                                                                                |
| G2  | **The three ownership claims for the `include!` refactor have zero implementations.** RFC-039 lists it under P4; `02` assigns the reference to itself; `06` also lists it under S7. However, the change list in `02` (6 additions + 20 modifications) contains **no `semantic_tokens.rs`**.                                                                                         | The implementation steps are assigned to §P5 5.1 of this document (after P4 is complete); references in RFC-039 and `06` now point here.                             |
| G3  | **Three concurrent inventories of CI scripts exist.** `RFC` lists four `scripts/ci/*.py`; `01` lists four scripts + one `cargo test`; `07` lists four checks with entirely different names; `04` also requires `check-synth-boundary.py`. After deduplication, there are **at least nine new scripts and no unified inventory**; `check-obligations.py` is owned by both P4 and P9. | The unified inventory appears in §P9 of this document (11 scripts, each owned by one phase). All other documents refer to that table.                                |
| G4  | **Conflict over the life of `precedence_inline.rs`.** `06` counts it among the “1,005 lines that must be revived,” while B6 in the same document says it “must be deleted together with the `Precedence` enum”; `05` argues for deletion.                                                                                                                                           | **Revive in P1 and delete with Pratt in P8** (decision registration C1)—first restore CI coverage, then perform the deletion as part of the dead-code ladder in 8.6. |
| G5  | **Conflicting statement of the L2 objective.** RFC-039 and this directory’s index say “grammar-driven syntax,” while `05` self-assesses: “**It did not adopt truly grammar-driven syntax (LALRPOP); it only achieved declarative table lookup.**”                                                                                                                                   | Consolidated into **fully grammar-driven LALRPOP syntax** (`05` §Syntax: Fully Grammar-Driven). The G7/G8 acceptance gates enforce that commitment.                  |

## Target Design: Phase Sequence

```
P0  仓库维护机制与代码放置规程     08        ← 新增，先立规矩
P1  复活孤儿测试并修缺陷           06 §S1
P2  建立等价性判据基线             07 全部
P3  修正确性漏洞（最小方案）        02 §S3-漏洞半部
P4  阶段契约与统一 Driver          02
P5  checker 文件内拆分             02 + 本文补齐
P6  类型表示单一化                 03
P7  中间表示 SSA 化                04      } 可并行
P8  前端范式变更                   05      }
P9  防反弹门禁（含统一脚本清单）     01 §防反弹 + 08 §可机检规则
P10 其余清理与状态修正             06 §S2/S3/S4/S5/S6
```

**Rationale for adding P0**: The three prohibitions—no fabrication, no endless filling, and no
patching when a refactor is required—constrain every action in P1–P10. The rules must be established
before work begins; otherwise, when P1 revives tests, work will already start adding `mod`
declarations to `tests/mod.rs`, which is precisely what caused the G4 conflict.

### P3 Fix Correctness Vulnerabilities (Minimal Solution)

Source: [02](02-stage-contract.md) §Implementation Points S3, vulnerability-fix half + **2026-10-07
silent-drop-point audit (3.4)**

> **Semantic-correction routing (D59, user decision on 2026-10-11)**: If this track or a later phase
> discovers a defect for which “the old behavior does not count as success”—**A silent failure / B
> silent mismatch / C undefined behavior and uncontrolled resources** (crashes, stack exhaustion,
> silently returning the wrong answer)—it **may be fixed alongside the refactor**, but all four
> requirements are mandatory: ① red before green (the red criterion must exist first and genuinely
> be red); ② no change to the externally observable behavior of programs that previously compiled
> successfully and ran correctly (they must not start failing, and their existing diagnostic
> codes/messages/exit codes must not be removed or changed); ③ registration in the same commit (this
> row identifies the error type + a revision note for that phase + the PR mandatory block’s “Error
> Type” field); ④ baseline regeneration in the same commit + manual review of the diff. **D
> user-visible semantics / E external-contract tables / F RFC-level design changes still require a
> user decision first.** See [07 §Semantic-Correction Routing Criteria](07-equivalence-oracle.md)
> for the expanded criteria and A–F whitelist; **the “default super-refactor + post-hoc
> registration” approach is not adopted** (see RFC-039 D59: without a red criterion, there is no way
> to distinguish a fix from a regression, and it conflicts with “C5′ does not exist” and “no
> relaxation whatsoever”).

      completed on 2026-10-25** (commit `4941f443`): the measured root cause was one layer deeper
      than stated in the issue—the typecheck decision loop could already select the correct
      candidate by name (proven by trace), and the false rejection came from the unknown-name
      **precheck** in the arity settlement block (it read the first candidate’s name table and
      ignored already-successful slot bindings); after the typecheck fix, ir_gen exposed a second
      layer (`lookup_param_info` looked up by leaf name in `fn_param_names`, with the last
      same-named overload overwriting earlier entries, so a decision hitting `Temp.pick` bound
      arguments using the parameter names of the last candidate `Temp.pick#1`). Fixed surface:
      precheck moved before slot binding, with the available column containing the union of all
      candidates’ parameter names; fallback path filters arity-compatible candidates by “name
      compatibility” (sharing `names_support_named_args` with the decision loop, a single criterion
      source), and the signature of the unique compatible candidate is emitted with it (E1002 is
      attributed to the candidate containing the name); ir_gen registers mixed names first and

**Acceptance**: Vulnerability criteria turn green, and **deliberately removing the fix must turn
them red again**. **Risk**: Adding E4018 to the multi-file path is a breaking change.

**3.4 acceptance**: 3.4.1 / 3.4.2 must have red criteria first (changing code first is forbidden);
3.4.3 is a manual review; 3.4.4–3.4.8 follow C2/C3 and P9’s 9.2 gate. **Risk**: The new diagnostic
in 3.4.1 is a behavioral change and requires the corpus differential baseline and CHANGELOG to be
updated in the same commit.

> **Hemorrhage-control path (decision on 2026-10-05)**: P3 does not have to wait for all of P2. The
> only hard prerequisites for 3.1/3.2 are **2.3.2 (multi-file corpus layer) + 2.4.1/2.4.3 (two red
> criteria)**; 2.1/2.2/2.3.1/2.3.3 (IR verifier, snapshots, single-file differential, performance
> baseline) can proceed in parallel with P3. Correctness bug remediation (such as `Sorted(3)`
> silently passing) must not be blocked by snapshot-infrastructure construction. 3.3
> (panic-to-diagnostic conversion) has no prerequisite and may land at any time.

### External Track RFC-029g: Remove `pub` and Auto-Binding (External Track / #399 — Ordering Constraint D56)

Source: [RFC-029g](../../rfc/accepted/029g-remove-pub-and-auto-bind.md) (accepted on 2026-10-02;
implementation not started), [#399](https://github.com/ChenXu233/YaoXiang/issues/399). **This track
is not a design item of the refactor itself; it is a specification-compliance deletion forced by an
ordering constraint**. It is distinct from P10 S5’s “reduce the visibility of `pub` items” (Rust
`pub(crate)` narrowing).

**Why it must be completed before P4**: 029g’s deletion surface overlaps the rewrite surface of four
refactor phases. Reversing the order means repeating the same deletions and baseline updates on the
new grammar / new checker layout / new SSA IR.

| 029g deletion surface                                               | Files                                                                                                              | Rewritten by which phase                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `KwPub` and syntax branches                                         | `lexer/{tokens,state,mod}.rs`; `parser/parser_state.rs`, `statements/{declarations,imports,functions,bindings}.rs` | **P8** (8.2 lexical convergence / 8.3 grammar construction / 8.6 Pratt deletion / 8.7 split of `parse_assign_after_target`)                                                                                                                                    |
| AST `is_pub` ×2 (`Assign` / `TypeDefinition`)                       | `parser/ast.rs:247/270`                                                                                            | **P6 6.5 AST dead variants** + P8 + the D1 directory rename (`parser/ast.rs` → top-level `src/ast/`)                                                                                                                                                           |
| `auto_bind_to_type` and the pub branch of `collect_exports`         | `typecheck/checker.rs:2150/3802/3836`, `environment.rs:284`                                                        | **P5** (the `include!`→real `mod` conversion and checker splitting are **pure moves**, so the pub branch is moved verbatim into the new files)                                                                                                                 |
| Dead-code exemptions `exempt_pub` / `is_exported` and role dispatch | `typecheck/passes/dead_code.rs`, `module/orchestrator.rs:389-407` (inside `check_project`)                         | **P4 4.2.2** (slimming `check_project`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `Public` modifier in `semantic_tokens`                              | `typecheck/checker/semantic_tokens.rs:450/498`                                                                     | **P4 4.2.6** + P5 + 4.6 (D55)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `is_pub`-ignoring binding in `ir_gen`                               | `middle/core/ir_gen.rs:1378`                                                                                       | **P7** (SSA conversion of `ir_gen.rs`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |

**Acceptance (behavioral changes, red criterion first)**:

1. **Red before green**: each of the two behavioral changes must first establish a red criterion
   before code changes—① `pub` changes from “silently accepted” to a parse error; ② an unreferenced
   top-level binding in a Lib/Script role newly reports W1001 (previously pub items were absolutely
   exempt).
2. **The test count must not decrease**: when deleting the `exempt_pub` case in
   `typecheck/passes/tests/dead_code.rs` and the `is_pub` fixtures, equivalent new cases must
   replace them.
3. **Baseline in the same commit**: diagnostic-set changes (the new parse error + the new W1001) ⇒
   the corpus differential baseline (`tests/baselines/corpus-parity.jsonl`) and CHANGELOG must be
   updated in the same commit. The IR snapshot is expected to have zero diff; if not, the reason
   must be explained.
4. **Independent revert unit**: the entire batch occupies one commit group and must not be mixed
   with other phases (DoD 6).
5. `cargo test` / `clippy -D warnings` / `python scripts/rfc/check_tracking.py` all pass.

**Ordering**: It overlaps with 3.4 (`checker.rs`, `environment.rs`) ⇒ the two must be sequential and
cannot run in parallel; **the entire track precedes P4** (RFC-039 D56). The 029g track does not
depend on 3.4; the implementer may choose which to start first based on available context.

> **Commit comparison (2026-10-08)**: When this section was incorporated (the P3.5 chapter + the
> main-chain/constraint rows) and RFC-039’s D56 were registered during the parallel-stream workspace
> cleanup, they were included together in `97071c81b`—whose commit message records “3.4 addendum
> registration + D57/D58 decisions archived” and does not mention this track. History is not
> rewritten; the fact is registered in place according to the established convention (`d5b18390`
> precedent, see the P2 implementation note), as a retrospective bisect reference.

|                             | 5.3.2 **Keep `collect_used_in_type` (`pub(crate)`) as `pub(crate)` under the `checker` module path**: it is a **shared-surface** function (consumed by both `collect_function_signature` and `inference/statements.rs:16`), and 5.3.1 **did not move it** in practice ⇒ the “zero caller changes” criterion **was already met**, so **do not add** a new `pub(crate) use annotations::*;` re-export. **Constraint retained**: if it is moved with the family in the future (into annotations or another later batch), `checker.rs` must add `pub(crate) use annotations::*;` to re-export it, leaving that import unchanged                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | 5.3.1        | **Zero caller changes** is this step’s hard acceptance criterion: `git diff src/frontend/core/typecheck/inference/statements.rs` is empty                                                                                                                      |

**Hard constraint**: `collect_used_in_type` is used across modules by `inference/statements.rs:16`.
**If** the function is moved with the annotation family in the future, **then** `checker.rs` must
add `pub(crate) use annotations::*;` to re-export it, leaving that import unchanged; 5.3 did not
move it in practice, so this batch added no re-export (the criterion was already met).

**Split only confirmed large blocks.** Do not split modules of only 200–400 lines—they add
navigation cost, reduce cohesion, and provide no decoupling benefit. The remaining submodules
(signatures / type_defs / imports) are to be split when the files grow back to 1,500 lines.

deleted variants, 6.4 only introduced injection, 6.5 only reshaped the representation, and 6.6 only changed gates, so **none of the four batches changed the IR shape**, and carrier disposal requires a separate round (the change would move IR snapshots and the corpus baseline)—**handled on 2026-11-01**: the `Instruction::Cast` / `TypeTest` carriers were removed with the two zero-producer dead variants in WBS 6.6.11 (`BytecodeInstr::Cast` is a different thing with the same name and is retained); `FunctionBody::TypeDecl.definition` was replaced with the IR-native narrowed carrier `TypeDeclBody` (field table + bytecode type-table entry) ⇒ **the IR layer has zero `ast::Type` carriers**; ⑥ **leg-B round-trip coverage must be committed**—the full-chain round trip from the reverse bridge B (`mono_to_ast_type`)’s output back through `From` to `MonoType` currently has coverage only from a probe test in the verification tree (not committed), so a separate implementation-side task must commit it formally; ⑦ **two built-in closed sets remain in the parser**—`src/frontend/core/parser/ast.rs:1084`’s `CONST_PARAM_TYPES` and `src/frontend/core/parser/probe.rs:121`’s `PREDICATE_NAMES` (`scripts/ci/check-boundary.py`’s B6 inventory has 2 entries); the injection seam is already in place (`src/driver/helpers.rs:1317`’s production use of `parse_with_probe`), so the default `parse()` entry point (`src/frontend/core/parser/probe.rs` records 9 production entry points) must be evaluated for injecting `SemanticProbe` everywhere so that `BuiltinProbe`’s built-in closed sets can be deleted; ⑧ the ranges of `substitute_type_ast` / `substitute_type_in_ast` are AST-derived, so documentation and prose must not hard-code line numbers—(**6.6.11 / 6.6.12 deleted both**, invalidating this item); ⑨ **snapshot coverage ≠ corpus file count** (**completed**, 6.6.16): **the regression entry point = t4 (`28ded1fd8`)**—that commit moved the parser’s three built-in name tables out of L2, and **all 9 production entry points injected `SemanticProbe`, except the snapshot criterion**: `middle/core/tests/snapshot.rs::normalize_corpus_file` still uses bare `parse()` (`NullProbe`) ⇒ `Terminates` is no longer recognized as a predicate ⇒ the return-position measure parameter is removed as a const generic parameter ⇒ `ir_gen` reports `E3006` (“variable b could not be resolved”) ⇒ canonicalized text returns `None` ⇒ the criterion **silently `continue`s**. **Measured baseline**: before t4, **216/216 full coverage, 0 skipped**; after t4, **214/218** (4 skipped: `deep_recursion_within_limit` / `explicit_termination_measure` / `measure_decreasing` / `stack_overflow_e6004`). The statements “`corpus-parity.jsonl:133` records `diagnostics: [] / exit: 0`” and the snapshot path’s E3006 do not mean that “the two criteria reach opposite conclusions for the same file”—the corpus baseline is produced by CLI/driver, and the driver already injects the probe, so the snapshot criterion itself supplied the wrong AST. **Both original errors were corrected**: ① it was not “pre-existing / present since 2026-10-12”; ② the proposed option “fix ir_gen’s E3006 path” was **withdrawn**—ir_gen is correct to report the error; the criterion supplied the wrong AST. **Fix** (change only the criterion, with zero product-code changes): change the entry point to `parse_with_probe(&tokens, &SemanticProbe)` (the same criteria as the driver); replace the relaxed `assert!(compared > 200)` lower bound with a **reconciliation assertion**—every case for which `reaches_ir_generation` is true but canonicalized text cannot be obtained **must fail and be named individually**, and assert `compared == reachable`; the update process must also make “skipped” red (otherwise stale snapshots are neither compared nor updated). After the fix, **218/218**, and `measure_decreasing.snap` converged to the narrowed-carrier shape (`type_decl []`—that type body contains only a const-bound expression and no data fields), with zero span residue in the snapshot surface. Measurement: restoring the entry point to bare `parse()` ⇒ the new assertion turns red and lists the 4 files above (coverage 214/218) |

**6.9 Model and Invariants (first-principles rewrite, 2026-11-13)**

> **Model**: D62’s end state is a **unified item world**—type positions and value positions share
> one set of compile-time items. One strength declaration produces one quantifier reading
> `q ∈ {0,1,ω}`; `q` is the **sole input** to slot allocation, and call-site argument views and
> implementation-header binding views are two projections of the same fact. Equality is a **pure
> decision** (after normalizing both sides, they are identical; no type variables are bound);
> evidence is the **sole entry point** for q=0 slots (four sources: literals/value
> environment/single-value pinning/Γ equalities); erasure is the **runtime consequence** of q=0
> (holding for both closed and open/recursive forms); Σ’s construction points **reuse** the same
> evidence interface rather than defining a separate one. **Completion criterion = the invariants
> hold, not that subphase names have run.**

| I2 Pure equality          | defeq does not bind variables; different Refined predicates ⇒ Unproven; criterion points first unify, and after failure roll back all bindings before defeq; one budget per decision | 6.9.3a/b/c’s kernel established; **6.9.3d connected to production criterion points** (`solver.unify`’s unify→rollback→defeq; the rollback and budget protocol were documented as part of d, see the commit history §P6 6.9.3d implementation round §2)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | B     |

> **6.9 closure criterion**: I1–I5 and I7 all hold; I6 is closed according to the 6.9.5 design
> review (does not block P7); I8 has evidence for every slice; residual items
> #537/#539/#540/#541/#542/#552 all have dispositions (fix / explicit exclusion / separate case).
> **Do not substitute “all subphases checked off” for the invariants holding.**

> **Partial order (not a serial chain)**: A facts → {A.C3, B.d, C.2, D design}; B kernel → {B.d,
> C.2, D implementation}; C.1 is independent and complete; F4 decision → A.C3; criterion-point
> protocol → B.d; design review → D implementation. A.C3 and B.d may proceed in parallel but require
> file-surface coordination (C3 leans toward typecheck→mono, d toward types/solver→sema), and C.2
> may proceed in parallel with B.d after 6.9.2-B/6.9.3a (it does not depend on defeq); D’s
> implementation waits for confirmation of the B design package; it depends on A (q=0 erasure) and
> C.1 (dispatch), and may depend on 6.9.3’s typed exit. **P7 gate = A/B/C exits; D does not block.**

> **A.C3’s prerequisite: the F4 decision (decided as A on 2026-11-16; measurements see the commit history §P6
> 6.9.2-C3 F4)**: the old wording, “`CheckOnly` evidence does not enter requests,” is
> obsolete—`ConstEvidence`/`CheckOnly` have been deleted; the current mechanism is that an open
> item’s `normalize` returns `Unproven` ⇒ no request is created, and the representation layer’s
> `GenericArg::Const(ConstExpr)` can hold `k-1`. **The decision changed to two orthogonal
> decisions + one termination subdecision**: D-a representation (A: open items enter `deferred` and
> are normalized before enqueueing / B: no specialization); D-b evaluation point (only mono is
> possible; C-1: a second field and C-2: reconstruction from IR both break the single
> representation/evaluator, C-3 = A); **F4-T termination** (naive firing pushes the recursive chain
> into `max_depth` and triggers E3005; T-a: fold within the instance / T-b: bounded firing + return
> to the original at the boundary / T-c: a termination layer or specification change). **First
> perform the M1–M6 measurements see the commit history §P6 6.9.2-C3 F4, then decide; before measurement, “A is
> already implementable” must not be treated as a conclusion.** **User decision A on 2026-11-16:
> D-a=A-closed, D-b=C-3, F4-T=T-b, bound=8; C3 implementation is a separate task, and the complete
> form of I4 is not claimed.**

> **B.d’s prerequisite: criterion-point protocol (not yet written)**: RFC-027 §12.1 specifies
> “criterion points first use unification; after unified failure, definitional equality may be used,
> but all bindings produced by the unification attempt must first be rolled back.” The
> implementation must answer: is the entry point an outer wrapper around `solver.unify` or a new
> `decide` layer; may defeq be attempted only when both sides are ground (have no unbound
> variables); what are the snapshot/restoration boundaries and costs; and do unify and defeq share
> one budget or have separate budgets. **Responsibility boundary**: unify continues to handle
> subtype/Any/Never; defeq only decides equivalence and does not take over subtyping, and
> bidirectional subtyping must not be treated as equivalence (RFC-027 §12.1). **First produce the
> protocol and production call graph, then write code**; 6.9.3c’s wiring contract was wasted for two
> rounds by assuming `can_assign` was the criterion point, while `AssignmentChecker` has zero
> production instantiations.

> **E acceptance and residuals (I8, cross-cutting)**: every slice must provide ① corpus/IR-snapshot
> zero drift or itemized classification; ② `testsuite run fast` + affected integration tests; ③
> error-code-generator idempotence (if an emission point/code table changes); and ④ classification
> of behavioral changes under D59 with red before green. **Wiring tasks additionally require**:
> production call-graph evidence (which production entry point, how many paths, which path is
> reachable); unit-test reachability must not be passed off as user reachability; rollback and
> budget must have measured figures. **Residual disposition**: #537 (class D, silent dropping of
> proof-function parameter refinements; do not fix before the user’s decision, and 6.9.4/6.9.5 must
> not depend on its fix), #539 (import-surface q=0; a C-phase boundary, and if it remains open,
> 6.9.4 covers only module-internal forms), #540 (deep-nesting stack overflow; **closed**—3.20.30
> closed on 2026-11-14: orchestration-layer 64 MB large-stack thread + parser depth gate
> MAX_PARSE_DEPTH=1024 + E0019, see row 09’s 3.20.30), #541 (TailCall; **closed**—on 2026-11-16,
> three locations advanced and retreated together: `call_form_is_rewritten` /
> `replace_calls_in_function` / `drop_erased_call_args` now cover `Call`/`TailCall` through the
> single point `rewritten_call_target`, with the init section and function body sharing one
> call-site rewrite; red→green + three mutations; see the commit history §P6 6.9.2 #541), #542 (isolated shared
> layer; **scheduled separately**—user decision B on 2026-11-16: schedule it **after P7 batch a and
> before wiring the post-mono production gate** for DCE; do not currently delete the original/shared
> layer, do not write a D38 exemption, and do not move the gate timing; 6.9 **remains open** pending
> completion. Evidence and review points are see the commit history §P6 6.9.2 #542), and #552 (proof execution for
> imported multi-parameter predicates; adjacent to 6.9.5, separate case). **Ownership pending
> decision**: `rfc027_refined_transparency`’s four open-constraint tripwires (`Eq(N, n)`) do not
> belong to 6.9.4’s constant-equality bridge; decide whether they remain in 6.9.4’s outOfScope or
> move to 6.9.1/E4018’s proof-function surface.

> **Non-claims/boundaries**: this item covers only record Σ (B: witness field), does not touch
> Π/`DepFn`, and does not claim the complete form of Π/Σ; 6.9.4 implements only constant equalities
> and does not fulfill the tripwires for open constraints `Eq(N,n)`; `f(y)(y)`’s second value
> position is not in this item; witness erasure ≠ Runtime check erasure; if #537 is not fixed,
> 6.9.4/6.9.5 cover only shapes that do not depend on proof-function parameter refinement; if #539
> is not fixed, 6.9.4 covers only module-internal forms; #540 is closed (3.20.30); #542 must land or
> be exempted before `verify` becomes a gate.

**Hard acceptance** (**criteria revised on 2026-10-27, carrying forward decision ② from
2026-10-26**): `git diff --stat tests/yaoxiang tests/baselines src/std/` must be **empty in every
round of 6.1–6.7**. **Direct-drive test files under `tests/`** (tests that directly drive changed
code, such as `tests/formatter_integration.rs`) may be modified when mechanically required, but each
change must be registered with **file / line count / reason**; **tests must not be deleted and
assertions must not be relaxed**. **Exception**: 6.8.2 is a **behavioral-fix** batch (new diagnostic
code + baseline update), does not fall under this hard acceptance criterion, and is a separate round
with a separate decision.

> **Revision relationship**: the original criteria (decision ② on 2026-10-26) were written as
> `git diff --stat tests/ src/std/`, treating all of `tests/` as zero-diff; the revised criteria
> **change only direct-drive test files from “zero diff” to “changeable but requiring file /
> line-count / reason registration”**; the zero-diff requirements for the corpus layer
> (`tests/yaoxiang`), baseline layer (`tests/baselines`), and standard library (`src/std/`) are
> unchanged. First application = 6.1.2: only `tests/formatter_integration.rs` changed, by **3
> lines** (2 `assert_format_eq` occurrences of `i64`→`Int64` + 1 comment line), reason = the
> expected type name in the assertion must be synchronized with the changed spelling; no test was
> deleted and no assertion was relaxed. See the commit history “P6 6.1 Name Canonicalization Closure.”

> **7.0 Design basis and boundaries**: the target layout comes from [01-routing](01-routing.md)’s
> directory tree and migration table—`middle/ir/ir_gen.rs` → `middle/lower/`,
> `sema/inference/expressions.rs` → `sema/infer/` (call-decision family `call/`). This batch
> performs **only pure moves**, with acceptance = “zero semantic diff except for paths” (manual
> review file by file) + C1 snapshot zero-diff. It does not conflict with RFC-039 alternative A’s
> rejection of “split files without changing the paradigm”: the rejected approach used moving
> **instead of** paradigm work, while this batch is a **prerequisite** for paradigm work—7c.1’s
> `lower/synth.rs` depends on the `lower/` directory existing, and 7d.1’s method-level split of
> `generate_call_expr_ir` is performed on smaller files after the move. On the expressions side, the
> user chose option A (split only the call-decision family; leave `statements.rs` in place); the
> wholesale `inference/ → infer/` rename and merging statements belong to a later design round after
> P7. `synth.rs` is a new boundary rather than a move and belongs to 7c.1. Survey baseline
> (2026-11-12): `ir_gen.rs` 9,804 lines, 148 methods, with the main body in one
> `impl AstToIrGenerator` block (about 9,250 lines); `expressions.rs` 6,825 lines, 93 methods, with
> the main body in one `impl ExpressionInferrer` block (about 6,280 lines); the largest methods are
> `infer_call_expr` at 1,671 lines and `generate_call_expr_ir` at 866 lines (the latter is 7d.1’s
> target).

**Risk**: 8.4’s dual-parser differential is an **unskippable equivalence gate**. If it finds
non-equivalence, the problem must be solved before 8.5 switches traffic, rather than hidden after
the switch by C5′. Missing any of the roughly 89 BP-reference migrations in 8.6 would **silently
change associativity** (no error, only a different parse result).

> **8.3–8.6 depend on D0/D2 from `08`**: if the grammar file is placed outside `parser/`, or the
> action code refers back to `sema`, `check-boundary.py` will block it.

### P9 Anti-regression Gates (Unified Script Inventory — This Document Fills Original Gap G3)

Source: [01](01-routing.md) §Anti-regression Mechanism + [08](08-maintenance-mechanism.md)
§Machine-Checkable Rules

**This is the sole authoritative inventory of all CI scripts** (11 scripts). Scripts are connected
to CI at their “introduction phase”; those marked report-only are converted to hard gates in P9
(from “report” to “fail”)—existing violations (parallel representations, disambiguation aliases,
etc.) are cleared only in P6/P8, and converting them to hard gates earlier would leave CI
permanently red and cause gates to be manually bypassed; when P9 converts them to hard gates, the
gates should be exactly green. P2’s 2.5, P4’s 4.3.2, and P7’s 7c.1 all refer to this table and do
not define separate inventories.

- [ ] **9.1–9.11 Connect the eleven scripts at their introduction phases and convert them to hard
      gates in P9** (details in the table below)
- [x] **9.12 Prerequisite for conversion to hard gates: rebaseline criterion C’s wiring points and
      clear the stale inventory** (2 Level 3 tasks, **registered and closed on 2026-10-26**): the
      title used at registration, “clear existing duplicate definitions,” was a
      **misnomer**—`--strict` violations come only from criterion C (entry-point wiring), while
      criterion A (duplicate definitions, 181 entries) is report-only and never makes `--strict`
      red; this was corrected at closure. Root cause: `STAGE_MARKERS` was calibrated on 2026-10-06
      against the pre-P4 state of “six paths wired manually,” and its comment explicitly says “after
      P4 unifies the Driver, this table is deleted with the inventory,” but after P4 landed, neither
      the vocabulary nor the 19 `KNOWN_WIRING_SITES` entries were retired. The criterion therefore
      classified the Driver’s **own stage table** (`Stage::DeadCodeAnalysis` variant name, `slug`
      string table, `state.rs` dependency table) as violations, exactly opposite to prohibition 3
      criterion C’s text (“adding a Nth entry **rather than registering it in the declarative stage
      table** → violation”). Disposition = **rebaseline the criterion** rather than add a whitelist.
      Result: `--strict` violations 9 → 0, inventory 19 → **7**, measured wiring points 18 → **8**
      (7 inventory + 1 dispatch point), criterion-A duplicate definitions 181 → 180 (deleting the
      duplicate `build_registry` entry), and `pub(crate)` 167 → 166. **Owning phase: P9; Blocks the
      mainline: No (blocks P9 conversion to hard gates).**
  - 9.12.1 Rebaseline delivery (four commits, itemized in the commit history’s closure
    record): ① **criterion rebaseline**—LEX merged into `Parsing` (`src/driver/stage.rs` and 02
    §Target Design 1 both state that `Parsing` = “lexing + syntax analysis (lex → parse)”),
    DEADCODE/MONO recognize only call forms, `#[cfg(test)] mod` is exempt by **brace-pair scope**,
    `dispatch` is listed separately as `DECLARED_DISPATCH_POINTS`, and 13 stale inventory entries
    were cleared (each with a removal reason, classified by measurement: 5 deleted functions / 5
    functions that still exist but have no stage primitive / 3 entries reduced to a single stage
    only because LEX was merged); ② **arm shapes become derived**—the `self.<arm>(` forms of 12
    variants are derived from `dispatch`’s exhaustive match (`Stage::ALL` supplies the variants, a
    single source) + three fail-loud guards (variant source invalid / arm not found / arm has no
    `fn` definition), and a false positive was fixed where the signature line counted as the
    measured body, causing `fn check_project(` to match TC itself (`fn_body` strips the signature;
    the measured “≥2 → <2 stages” reduction was 0); ③ **coverage declarations become factual**—the
    phase implementations of 4 variants (VendorConsistency / Discovery / Registry /
    RoleClassification) are shared primitives outside the Driver and are truthfully registered as
    known blind spots; ④ **blind spots are classified by nature**—live wiring
    (`collect_package_refs`, called from `driver/arms.rs:626`) and dead-code candidates
    (`build_registry`, zero callers) cannot receive the same disposition. Three independent reviews
    all judged needs_revision (medium → medium → low, with all three findings genuinely fixed), and
    the fourth round **passed** with no findings. **Acceptance**:
    `python scripts/ci/check-fanout.py --strict` exits 0 (violations 0 / inventory 6 / dispatch
    point 1) ✅
  - 9.12.2 Expand criterion-C coverage and clear `build_registry` (**completed; closed on
    2026-10-26**; user decisions **D1-A retain + D2-A delete**): ① **delete** the zero-caller chain
    `build_registry` / `build_registry_from` (formerly `orchestrator.rs:379-396`) and the now
    callerless `discover` (formerly `:416-418`); `DiscoveredFile` / `Discovery` /
    `extract_module_info` / `parse_file` are all retained; because `build_registry` was a lib-target
    public item through `src/lib.rs:27 pub mod frontend` →
    `frontend/module/mod.rs:20 pub mod orchestrator`, its deletion was separately decided as an
    external API-surface change. ② **retain** the stage primitives outside the Driver that still
    exist after deletion: VendorConsistency ← `ensure_vendor_consistency`, Discovery ←
    `discover_with_used`, RoleClassification ← `roles::classify` (narrowed to the qualified form—a
    bare name would falsely catch same-named helpers in other modules, and the constructed
    counterexample is reproducible), and Registry ← `extract_module_info` (do not use bare
    `.register(`, which occurs under multiple names in the repository). ③ Exactly one newly exposed
    item was found: `collect_package_refs` (`driver/helpers.rs:1057`, live code, called from
    `driver/arms.rs:626`), registered as the inventory, increasing it 6 → 7 and measured wiring
    points 7 → 8. ④ The criterion docstring’s coverage declaration was changed to facts: the old
    “6+6” split was merged into **10+2**, R1–R3’s decidable retention criteria were added, “every
    function that constructs that phase’s data is not retained” was deleted because 5 table entries
    disprove it, and three stale line numbers/unmeasured figures and two false comments were fixed
    (`orchestrator.rs:7` module header, `statements.rs:737` reference to a deleted function).
    Behavior-neutral: corpus 356 lines byte-identical, 216 snapshots with zero drift,
    `cargo test --lib` 2855/0/2, and `pub(crate)` 167 → 166 (the necessary result of deleting
    `discover`). Review chain: round 1 needs_revision (1 medium + 2 low) → fixes → round 2
    **passed** (independently reproduced with a self-made probe); then the F4/F5/F6/F8 cleanup round
    → narrow re-review **passed** (AST-based line-by-line comparison confirmed no incidental
    changes).

## Implementation Points: Dependencies and Parallelization

### Mandatory Serial Main Chain

`P0 → P1 → P2 → P3 → P3.5 → P4 → P5 → P6 → (P7 ∥ P8) → P9 → P10`

> **P3 hemorrhage-control path (decision on 2026-10-05)**: P3’s prerequisites are narrowed to
> **2.3.2 + 2.4.1 + 2.4.3** (multi-file corpus layer + two red criteria). The rest of P2 (2.1 IR
> verifier, 2.2 snapshots, 2.3.1/2.3.3 differential and performance baseline) can proceed in
> parallel with P3. Correctness remediation is not blocked by criterion-infrastructure construction;
> however, **P4 must still wait for all of P2**—the unified Driver needs all three criterion layers
> in place.

| Constraint         | Reason                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| ------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| P0 → P1            | The three prohibitions constrain every P1 action (1.2.x adds `mod` declarations to `mod.rs`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| P1 → P2            | Reviving tests changes the test count and corpus baseline                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| P2 (narrowed) → P3 | The vulnerability criteria must be **written as red first** (2.4.1/2.4.3), and the multi-file corpus layer (2.3.2) must exist so the red tests have somewhere to run                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| P3 → P4            | Fix bugs before refactoring; the reverse order would freeze bugs into the stage table as “established behavior”                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| P3.5 → P4          | RFC-029g (#399)’s deletion surface overlaps the rewrite targets of P4 4.2.2 / P5 / P6 / P7 / P8 (the list appears in this document’s §P3.5)—the reverse order means repeating the same deletions on the new grammar, checker layout, and IR (D56)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| P4 → P5            | The `include!` refactor and checker split change the same file                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| P5 → P6            | Consecutive changes to the same file must be separated, or regressions cannot be located by bisection                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| P6 → P7 / P8       | If type representations do not converge first, the new IR grows into a third representation; **P7 gate = exits A/B/C of 6.9** (I1–I3, I5, I7; namely 6.9.2 including C3, 6.9.3 including d, and completed 6.9.4), while D (Σ) does not block; P7 may consume only quantifier-derived views and the defeq interface and must not create a parallel ledger; the 7.0 pure-move batch is a P7 prerequisite and is not subject to this gate, but it must not consume the undecided defeq/quantifier interfaces |
| P7/P8 → P9         | The 9.5 snapshot baseline and 9.3 `pub(crate)` leak count reach their final form only after P7/P8                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |

### Parallelizable Groups

| Group                                        | Members                                                                            | Rationale                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| -------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **A (the only group authorized by RFC-039)** | P7 ∥ P8                                                                            | Disjoint file sets: `ir.rs`/`ir_gen.rs`/`bytecode.rs`/`translator.rs` vs `lexer/*`/`parser/*`                                                                                                                                                         |
| B                                            | 1.2.1 ∥ 1.2.2 ∥ 1.2.3 ∥ 1.2.4                                                      | Each independently adds `mod`; revert file by file                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| C                                            | 2.1 ∥ 2.2 ∥ 2.3.1 ∥ 2.3.2 ∥ 2.4                                                    | The three criterion layers are mutually independent                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| D                                            | 4.4 (proof-layer closure) ∥ the entire P6 line                                     | `02` explicitly states in S5 that it “should proceed independently of S1–S4”                                                                                                                                                                          |
| E                                            | 4.2.5 ∥ 4.2.6 ∥ 4.2.8                                                              | All are mutually independent after 4.1 completes                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| F                                            | 9.3 ∥ 9.9 ∥ 9.10 (the three scripts introduced in P0 may be developed in parallel) | The 9.5 snapshot baseline must be taken last                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| G                                            | S3 ∥ S4 ∥ S5                                                                       | Disjoint file sets (verified on 2026-10-05: S3 changes `docs/src/dev/design/check/` + `config.js`; S4 changes `docs/src/rfc/` + the `check_tracking.py` generator; S5 changes two `pub` items under `src/frontend/core/typecheck/`; no pair overlaps) |

**Non-parallelizable (file overlap)**: P1(1.1.x) ∥ P8(8.0.x); 1.2.1 ∥ 8.3.2; 6.0 ∥ S2 (same files in
the B6/E5 batch); 4.3.2 ∥ 9.2 (now unified under P4); **3.4 ∥ P3.5** (the same files, `checker.rs` /
`environment.rs`—the external track’s pub-deletion batch and the silent-channel fixes must be
sequential).

**New internal document constraints**: 6.0→6.1→6.2→6.3→6.4→6.5→6.6 is strictly sequential; 7.0 →
7a→7b→7c→7d is strictly sequential (7.0 is P7’s prerequisite pure-move batch), and 7b’s `Phi` arm
must precede 7d.

## Key Decisions and Rationale

- **P0 is an independent phase rather than part of P9**—gates are “after-the-fact checks,” whereas
  rules are “before-the-fact judgment.” P9’s scripts cannot prevent “patching when a refactor was
  required”; only the D0–D4 review checklist can.
- **P9 is the sole authoritative inventory of CI scripts**—the original G3 gap’s three concurrent
  inventories prevented closure. This table consolidates them into 11 scripts, each owned by one
  phase, with report-only status and conversion timing explicitly traceable.
- **P3.5 incorporates RFC-029g rather than leaving it as an “independent track to execute after the
  refactor”**—its deletion surface overlaps the rewrite surface of P4/P5/P6/P7/P8 (RFC-039 D56).
  Deferring it until after the refactor means repeating the same deletions on the new grammar,
  checker layout, and IR; this is the same lesson as incorporating 2315/2341 into 7e and correcting
  RFC status in S4.
- **P5’s steps are supplied by this document rather than merged into P4**—P4 is already the largest
  single-point risk, and adding the checker split would exceed the rollback granularity.
- **5.3.2 uses “zero caller changes” as hard acceptance**—the split must be independently
  revertible.

## Known Limitations and Risks

- **1.3.x (fixing exposed defects) has no regression criterion**—it only says “the fixed defect must
  not be rolled back” and lacks a requirement to record what was fixed. **Execution convention**:
  every 1.3.x fix commit message must reference the name of the fixed test and a description of the
  defect, as a retrospective bisect reference.
- ~~**7b.1 is too coarse**~~ **Fixed on 2026-10-05**: split into 7b.1a / 7b.1b as two exclusive
  commits under DoD 6.
- **8.2.1 is too coarse**—about 89 references are renamed, and missing one would **silently change
  associativity**.
- ~~**D17 / D20 have no corresponding Level 3 tasks**~~ **Fixed on 2026-10-05**: D17 lands as 7e.1,
  D20 lands as 4.5.1/4.5.2; D32/D34 opcode reclamation is merged into 7e.1, and the deletion actions
  remain in P10 S6.
- ~~**The `2315` / `2341` data-loss defects were originally assigned to an “independent issue”**~~
  **Fixed on 2026-10-05**: incorporated as 7e.2 / 7e.3. The exception table (throw/try) is core
  language semantics and must not be left as a deferred implementation legacy.
- ~~**E2 (`lib.rs:568/582` `todo!()`) has no phase ownership**~~ **Fixed on 2026-10-05**: merged
  into P10 S2.
- ~~**F7 (opcode-gate extractor) has ambiguous ownership**~~ **Fixed on 2026-10-05**: merged into
  P10 S6’s generation-time gate task.

## Conflict Registration: All Decisions Made

> **The original “Conflict Registration” C1–C8 and the open questions in the individual documents
> have all converged into the
> [RFC-039 Decision Register](../../rfc/accepted/039-compiler-architecture.md) (D1–D50).** This
> document retains no pending items. The one-to-one mapping is:

| Original Number | Issue                                                                   | Decision                                                                                                                                      |
| --------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| C1              | `precedence_inline.rs` is revived in P1 and deleted in P8               | **Revive** (P1’s premise is that revival restores real assertions), and P8’s 8.6 deletes the file together with Pratt                         |
| C2              | “Grammar-driven” commitment vs table lookup                             | **Fully implemented**: LALRPOP is used; see `05` “Syntax: Fully Grammar-Driven”                                                               |
| C3              | Conflict between C6’s category definition and S1 classification         | **S1 belongs to C6** (revival needs no equivalence criterion, only regression tests); C6 is expanded to “pure deletion or pure revival”       |
| C4              | `test_release_plan_spans_consumed` whitelist                            | **No whitelist**; the difference must be empty; if it cannot be empty, the `ReleasePlan` contract is defective ⇒ change to `PlanId` (D20/D41) |
| C5              | Unverified internal differences between the two `Aggregation` functions | **P4’s 4.2.7 makes this a mandatory verification item**, to be confirmed line by line during implementation                                   |
| C6              | The wasm playground fix requires P4’s `ProgramKind`                     | **The wasm-path bug fix is merged into 4.2.8**; P3 handles only the three native entry points                                                 |
| C7              | Ownership of `method_def_ordinals`                                      | **Belongs to 04** (P7 batch c), see D21                                                                                                       |
| C8              | `upvalue_count` correction and the `.42` version number                 | **Fix + change `VERSION` from 4 to 5** (the format header already has `MAGIC` + `VERSION` fields, `codegen/bytecode.rs:14-16`), see D17       |
